> DEVELOPER

Implement the following plan:

# FLUME VAE: Red Flags → Green Lights

## Core Principles

### 1. Learn Before You Burn

**No destructive operations without extracting knowledge first.** Before removing, replacing, or deprecating any code, data, or artifact:

1. **Identify patterns** — What good ideas exist in the code being removed? What design decisions were sound even if the implementation failed?
2. **Identify anti-patterns** — What went wrong? What assumptions were incorrect? What traps should future work avoid?
3. **Store in vault** — Log all patterns via `vault_extract_pattern` and anti-patterns via `vault_log_decision` to the MCP vault. Knowledge compounds; deleted code doesn't.
4. **Only then proceed** with removal.

This applies to every phase of this spec, not just Sprint 0. When Phase 2 replaces Phase 1 components, the same discipline applies.

### 2. Test-Driven Development (Red → Green → Refactor)

**Every feature starts with a failing test.** No production code without seeing RED first.

| Step | Action | Verification |
|------|--------|-------------|
| **RED** | Write a test that describes the desired behavior. Run it. Watch it fail. | Test fails for the *right reason* (missing feature, not syntax error) |
| **GREEN** | Write the minimum code to make the test pass. Nothing more. | Test passes. All existing tests still pass. |
| **REFACTOR** | Improve code quality while keeping tests green. | All tests still pass after changes. |

**Applied to FLUME:**
- Each VAE component (encoder, decoder, loss, training loop) gets its test FIRST
- Evaluation metrics get tests that verify known inputs produce expected outputs BEFORE implementation
- Integration points (SemanticCache, JourneyTracker) get contract tests BEFORE wiring

**Recovery rule:** If production code was written first (hook fires), write the test immediately — don't revert working code, but apply TDD properly going forward.

### 3. Compound Engineering

**Every feature makes future features easier.** The compound loop applies to this spec:

```
Implement feature → Validate manually → Write 5 tests → Extract learning → Refine skill
```

| Principle | Application in This Spec |
|-----------|-------------------------|
| **Knowledge compounds** | Every anti-pattern logged to vault prevents re-occurrence. Every pattern extracted is reusable. |
| **Build on proven templates** | New EmbeddingProvider follows existing `ExperienceCollector` multi-tier fallback pattern. New VAE loss follows existing `FlumeVAETrainer` structure (improved, not reinvented). |
| **Experience-guided execution** | Query vault for prior learnings before each sprint (lesson-37 validates this). |
| **Skill refinement loop** | After Sprint 3 evaluation, log results as vault experiment. Learnings flow back into Sprint 4 decisions. |
| **Honest metrics** | Report actual numbers (98.8% beats inflated 100%). Green-light criteria are pass/fail, not "close enough." |

### 4. Context Awareness

**Every session starts informed.** No cold starts.

| Checkpoint | Action |
|------------|--------|
| **Sprint start** | Query vault: `vault_find_relevant_context(query="FLUME VAE training")`. Read continuation file if exists. Check existing experiment notes. |
| **Before building** | Query Context7 for library docs (PyTorch VAE patterns, nomic-embed-text API). Don't assume — verify. |
| **Mid-sprint** | Check `cz context --json`. If >80%, prepare handoff. Write continuation with EXACT state. |
| **Sprint end** | Log to vault: decisions made, metrics achieved, patterns discovered. Next session starts informed. |

**Overnight simulation data** (5.5M trajectories) is a context asset. Log its characteristics to vault so Phase 2 sessions can find it without re-discovering.

### 5. Token Efficiency

**Budget tokens like money.** Based on CLAUDE.md token budgets:

| Task Type | Budget | Pattern |
|-----------|--------|---------|
| Implement 1 feature | 500-1,500 tokens | Copy template → code → manual test → 5 tests |
| Research + implement | 2,000-3,000 tokens | Quick survey → proof of concept → tests |
| Single test debug | 200-500 tokens | Add print → run → check → fix → verify |

**Anti-patterns to avoid:**
- Research-first (5,000-10,000 tokens for no implementation)
- Infrastructure for products that don't exist (8,000+ tokens)
- Running full test suite repeatedly (use `-k` to target specific tests)

**Efficiency tactics for this spec:**
- Use `uv run pytest tests/flume/ -k "test_vae"` not `uv run pytest tests/ -q` during development
- Cache Ollama embeddings in Sprint 1 LRU cache — avoid redundant API calls during training
- Batch embed texts (50 at a time) instead of one-by-one
- Mock Ollama in tests — never call live Ollama in unit tests
- Write narrow tests (one behavior per test) — faster to run, faster to debug

### 6. Evidence Over Assumptions

Every red flag became a red flag because someone assumed something worked without checking. Every claim in this spec has a concrete verification command. If you can't run a command to prove it, you can't claim it.

### 7. Phased with Hard Gates

No Phase 2 work begins until ALL Phase 1 green-light criteria are met with evidence. "Probably fine" is not a gate-pass.

---

## Context

The FLUME investigation (2026-02-23) identified 6 red flags — critical unknowns that determine whether FLUME is a viable research artifact or an architectural dead end. Codebase exploration revealed the situation is worse than the investigation assumed: **the system the investigation analyzed doesn't actually exist.** Two incompatible VAE implementations, a broken data pipeline training on random noise, and zero integration with agent behavior mean the red flags aren't "unknowns to measure" — they're **architectural problems to solve.**

This plan starts by freeing system resources (7 GB RAM from zombie processes, 7.4 GB disk from stale artifacts, 2,000+ lines of dead code), then rebuilds FLUME from first principles in two phases, turning each red flag green with concrete, verifiable criteria. It also maps FLUME's capabilities to the Anthropic Universes team Research Engineer role to validate strategic alignment.

```bash
free -h                                # Confirm RAM recovered
python -c "from cohezion.flume import FlumeVAEEncoder"  # Core import works
```

### Knowledge Extraction (BEFORE any deletions)

**Rule: No destructive operations without learning.** Before removing any code, extract patterns and anti-patterns into the MCP vault. This preserves institutional knowledge for future sessions.

**Anti-patterns to document (via `vault_log_decision` / `vault_extract_pattern`):**

| Anti-Pattern | Source File | Lesson |
|-------------|------------|--------|
| Dual VAE architecture | `autoencoder.py` + `training.py` | Two models operating on different input spaces creates integration debt that compounds silently. One coherent model > two partial ones. |
| Training on random noise | `dataset.py` (SyntheticFlumeDataset) | VAE trained on N(0.5, 0.15) learns Gaussian structure, not semantic structure. Synthetic data must have the *kind* of structure you want the model to learn. |
| SHA-256 as semantic embedding | `vae_encoder.py` + `experience_encoder.py` | Cryptographic hashes destroy semantic similarity. "deploy API" and "API deployment" hash to completely unrelated 256D vectors. Never use hash functions where semantic distance matters. |
| Hash-based journey tracking | `journey_tracker.py` | Deterministic text hashing for 12D trajectories means semantically identical tasks map to different points. Observable AI requires *meaningful* representations, not arbitrary ones. |
| Disconnected modules | morphospace, bioelectric, lcsp, etc. | 1,353 lines of code with zero consumers = pure maintenance burden. If a module has no imports, it's not "future work" — it's dead weight. Enforce "no orphan modules" policy. |
| HIHO coherence loss on batch mean | `training.py` line 118-120 | Penalizing `mean(μ) → 0.5` at batch level doesn't ensure per-sample coherence. Regularization must target the right granularity. |
| Character-level tokenizer | `tokenizer.py` + `autoencoder.py` | 74-char vocabulary for semantic embeddings produces poor representations. Always use subword tokenization (or better: pre-trained embeddings). |
| Zombie test processes | System observation | Pytest processes with async tests can hang indefinitely if event loops aren't properly torn down. Always set test timeouts (`pytest-timeout`). |

**Patterns worth preserving (good ideas in dead code):**

| Pattern | Source File | Worth Keeping |
|---------|------------|---------------|
| Morphospace stability wells | `morphospace.py` | Concept of "stability wells" around HIHO=0.5 in latent space is sound theory. Re-implement in Phase 2 with real learned latent space. |
| Trajectory prediction with physics | `predictor.py` | Momentum-based prediction and counterfactual branching (`imagine_branches()`) are good Phase 2 features. Preserve the API design. |
| Multi-tier data collection | `experience_collector.py` | Parquet → SurrealDB → Vault → synthetic fallback hierarchy is good architecture. Reuse in new data pipeline. |
| ExperienceEncoder layout | `experience_encoder.py` | The `[12D trajectory | 12 metrics | 5 op_type | ...]` prefix structure is well-designed. Keep for Phase 2, replace only the 227D fingerprint segment. |
| Checkpoint format with config | `training.py` | Saving `{model, optimizer, config, epoch, metrics}` together is correct practice. Carry forward to new training pipeline. |
| LCSP concept | `lcsp.py` | Latent Coherence Stability Predictor concept is valid for Phase 2 — predicting whether a latent point is in a stable region. |
| Bioelectric field modeling | `bioelectric.py` | Mapping latent gradients to "field strength" for action generation is creative. Document for Phase 2 investigation. |

**Vault storage plan:**
1. Log each anti-pattern as a `vault_log_decision` (decision: "don't do X because Y")
2. Extract each preservable pattern as a `vault_extract_pattern` (with code examples from the dead modules)
3. Log the overall cleanup as a `vault_log_experiment` (hypothesis: "dead code removal improves system health")

## Architecture: Fresh FLUME VAE

### Core Insight

Don't learn semantic embeddings from scratch with a tiny model. Use a **pre-trained embedding model** (Ollama `nomic-embed-text`, 768D) as input, then train a **proper VAE** to learn a structured, compressed 256D latent space on top of those embeddings.

### Phase 1 Architecture: Semantic Cache Engine

```
Text Input
    │
    ▼
[Ollama nomic-embed-text]  ─── 768D dense semantic embedding
    │
    ▼
[FLUME VAE Encoder]         ─── MLP: 768→512→512→384 → mu(256), logvar(256)
    │
    ▼
256D latent z (mu used as embedding at inference)
    │
    ▼
[FLUME VAE Decoder]         ─── MLP: 256→384→512→512→768 (reconstruct input)
    │
    ▼
[SemanticCache]             ─── cosine similarity matching (threshold ~0.88)
```

**Why this works:**
- Pre-trained embeddings already capture "deploy API" ≈ "API deployment"
- VAE learns *structured* 256D space where distances are meaningful
- KL regularization ensures smooth latent space (no holes between clusters)
- Decoder forces VAE to retain information (reconstruction validates quality)
- ~2.1M parameters — trains on CPU in minutes

### Loss Function

```
L = L_recon + β·L_KL + λ_align·L_contrastive + λ_coh·L_coherence

L_recon       = MSE(decoded, original_embedding)
L_KL          = -0.5·Σ(1 + logvar - μ² - exp(logvar))    with free-bits per dimension
L_contrastive = InfoNCE over known paraphrase pairs        (τ=0.07)
L_coherence   = (mean(μ) - 0.5)²                          HIHO target
```

**KL collapse prevention (Red Flag 1):**
- β annealing: 0.0 → 0.1 over first 30% of training steps
- Free-bits: min 0.125 bits/dimension — forces each dimension to encode information
- Active unit monitoring: alert if <128 of 256 dimensions are active

### Phase 2 Architecture: Reasoning Latent Space

Extends Phase 1 with a **temporal encoder** over trajectory sequences:

```
Trajectory Steps [(12D_state, metrics, op_type), ...]
    │
    ▼
[Step Embedding]       ─── Linear(29→128) + positional encoding
    │
    ▼
[Transformer Encoder]  ─── 2 layers, 4 heads, d_model=128
    │
    ▼
[Attention Pooling → mu(256), logvar(256)]
    │
    ▼
[Transformer Decoder]  ─── autoregressive trajectory reconstruction
```

Enables: interpolation between strategies, novel trajectory sampling, latent space navigation for agent guidance. JourneyTracker transitions from SHA-256 hashing to learned FLUME projections.

## Training Data Strategy (Hybrid)

### Bootstrap (Phase 1 — immediate)

1. **Structured synthetic tasks** (~5,000 samples)
   - Task descriptions across 5 operation types (generate, analyze, search, transform, persist)
   - Template groups with known paraphrases: `["deploy API", "API deployment", "launch API service"]`
   - Each embedded via `nomic-embed-text` → 768D vectors

2. **Codebase docstrings** (~2,000 samples)
   - Extract from Cohezion source via AST parsing
   - Real domain-specific language

3. **Vault experiment descriptions** (variable, ~500+ samples)
   - From `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/experiments/*.json`

4. **Paraphrase generation** (~15,000 pairs)
   - Use Ollama to generate 3 paraphrases per task template
   - Provides contrastive training pairs

### Fine-tune (accumulates over time)

5. **Real execution data pipeline**
   - After each compound execution: store `(task_description, operation_type, metrics, skill_name)` as JSONL
   - Path: `data/flume/experiences/execution_log.jsonl`
   - Periodic re-embed + re-train (weekly or on-demand)

### External (Phase 2)

6. **Published reasoning datasets** (GSM8K chains, MATH solutions, code generation traces)
   - For trajectory-level training in Phase 2

## Universes Team Alignment (Red Flag 6)

**Source:** [Research Engineer, Universes](https://job-boards.greenhouse.io/anthropic/jobs/5061517008)

### Mapping: Team Needs → FLUME Capabilities

| Universes Team Need | FLUME Capability | Alignment |
|---------------------|------------------|-----------|
| **Agentic training environments** for extended-duration tasks | Compound engineering loop: 11-step execution pipeline with skill refinement, retrospection, and journey tracking. Literally an RL training environment for agentic tasks. | **Strong** |
| **Evaluation frameworks measuring genuine capability gains** | Red Flag 4 resolution produces a validated evaluation framework with correlation-tested metrics. Phase 1 evaluation suite (reconstruction fidelity, similarity preservation, KL health, cache ablation) demonstrates evaluation engineering rigor. | **Strong** |
| **Navigate ambiguity, manage interruptions, sustain context** | Phase 2 reasoning latent space compresses reasoning trajectories for context sustainability. 256D embeddings preserve semantic meaning across long interactions. Morphospace navigation handles ambiguity via stability wells. | **Strong** (Phase 2) |
| **RL environments and simulation platforms** | 12D universe simulation with FLUME VAE as the representation layer. JourneyTracker records state transitions. DegradationDetector monitors drift. This is RL infrastructure. | **Strong** |
| **Integrate into production training systems** | Phase 1 semantic cache engine is a production system. Demonstrates ability to ship ML infrastructure, not just research prototypes. | **Strong** |
| **Rapidly troubleshoot across research and production ML** | This spec itself — diagnosing architectural fractures, designing fixes, implementing with TDD — demonstrates this capability directly. | **Meta** |

### Preferred Qualifications Match

| Qualification | Evidence |
|---------------|----------|
| LLM training/fine-tuning/evaluation | VAE training pipeline, evaluation framework, metric validation |
| RL environments, simulation platforms | 12D universe simulation, compound loop as RL env |
| Large-scale ML infrastructure | SemanticCache, ExperienceCollector, multi-tier data pipeline |
| Containerization, distributed systems | Cloud Run deployment, Tailscale Funnel for MCP, multi-session worktree pattern |

### Positioning Document (Deliverable)

Produce a concise document (max 2 pages) that:
1. Frames FLUME as an **agentic training environment** with validated evaluation
2. Highlights the **evaluation engineering** (not just the model)
3. Shows **production readiness** (Phase 1 cache engine in production)
4. Positions the **compound engineering loop** as an RL training methodology
5. Demonstrates research taste: honest red-flag identification, systematic resolution

### Key Integration Points

**`vae_encoder.py`** (primary integration surface):
```
Current: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D → MLP encoder → mu → 256D
New:     text → Ollama embed → 768D → VAE encoder → mu → 256D
         (fallback: text → SHA-256 hash → expand to 256D if Ollama unavailable)
```

**`semantic_cache.py`** (consumer — no interface changes):
```
FlumeVAEEmbeddingModel.encode(text) → EmbeddingResult(embedding=[...256 floats...])
# Same interface, better embeddings
```

**`journey_tracker.py`** (Phase 1 minimal change):
```
text_to_latent(text):
    if flume_encoder available:
        flume_256d = flume_encoder.encode(text)
        return np.tile(flume_256d, 8)  # 256×8 = 2048D for existing pipeline
    else:
        return hash_based_latent(text)  # existing behavior
```

## Verification Strategy

### Per Red Flag

| Red Flag | Verification Command/Method | Pass Criteria |
|----------|----------------------------|---------------|
| 1. KL collapse | `uv run python -m scripts.train_flume_v2 --evaluate-kl` | Active units ≥ 128/256, KL > 0.1 |
| 2. Reconstruction | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k reconstruction` | cos_sim(input, recon) > 0.95 on validation |
| 3. Synthetic quality | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k paraphrase` | Precision@1 > 0.85 |
| 4. Metrics validation | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k similarity_preservation` | Spearman ρ > 0.90 between original and latent similarities |
| 5. Integration impact | `uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -k cache_ablation` | FLUME precision-recall dominates hash baseline |
| 6. Team fit | Read positioning document, verify 3+ alignment points | Qualitative: convincing narrative |

### Full Suite

```bash
# Unit tests
uv run pytest tests/flume/ -v

# Train and evaluate
uv run python scripts/train_flume_v2.py --config default --evaluate

# Integration test
uv run pytest tests/flume/test_evaluate_vae.py -v

# Existing test suite (no regressions)
uv run pytest tests/ -q
```

## Adversarial Review Findings

Three-perspective review per vault protocol (ADR 2026-02-14). All findings addressed in the plan above.

### Feasibility Challenger

| Finding | Severity | Resolution |
|---------|----------|------------|
| `nomic-embed-text` not installed in Ollama | HIGH | Added Task 0b: `ollama pull nomic-embed-text` |
| 768D→256D is only 3x compression — bottleneck may not force meaningful structure learning | MEDIUM | Contrastive loss (InfoNCE) provides additional learning signal beyond reconstruction. If bottleneck proves insufficient, reduce decoder capacity or add dropout. Monitor active units. |
| ~22,500 training samples for 2.1M params = overfitting risk | MEDIUM | Weight decay (1e-4), dropout (0.1), early stopping (patience=10), and data augmentation (paraphrases). Phase 1 is a starting point; real data accumulates. |
| Ollama network dependency at inference | MEDIUM | Fallback chain in EmbeddingProvider: Ollama → cached embeddings → SHA-256 hash. Cache hot embeddings in LRU cache (text→768D). |
| Paraphrase generation quality depends on Ollama model | LOW | Use simple templates + programmatic augmentation (word reordering, synonym substitution) before LLM generation. LLM paraphrases are supplementary, not primary. |

### Strategic Reviewer

| Finding | Severity | Resolution |
|---------|----------|------------|
| Phase 1 (cache engine) doesn't demonstrate RL capability — weak positioning | HIGH | Move positioning document to Sprint 1 (not Sprint 4). Frame Phase 1 as "evaluation engineering rigor" and Phase 2 as "RL environment." The investigation and systematic red-flag resolution ARE the demonstration of research engineering quality. |
| Overnight simulation data (5.5M trajectories) = irreplaceable Phase 2 training data | CRITICAL | Changed from "delete" to "compress and preserve." Added data characterization task. |
| Green-light criteria are internally defined — may not match what Universes team values | MEDIUM | Positioning document (moved to Sprint 1) explicitly maps our metrics to team needs. Adjust criteria if mapping reveals misalignment. |

     1→"""FLUME VAE v2 — Semantic latent space from pre-trained embeddings.
     2→
     3→Architecture:
     4→  Encoder: 768 → 512 → 512 → 384 → mu(256), logvar(256)
     5→  Decoder: 256 → 384 → 512 → 512 → 768
     6→
     7→Loss: L_recon + β·L_KL (with free-bits) + λ_coh·L_coherence
     8→"""
     9→
    10→from __future__ import annotations
    11→
    12→import torch
    13→import torch.nn as nn
    14→import torch.nn.functional as F  # noqa: N812
    15→
    16→
    17→class FlumeVAE(nn.Module):
    18→    """Variational autoencoder for semantic embedding compression.
    19→
    20→    Takes pre-trained 768D embeddings (e.g., nomic-embed-text) and learns a
    21→    structured 256D latent space where distances are semantically meaningful.
    22→    """
    23→
    24→    def __init__(
    25→        self,
    26→        input_dim: int = 768,
    27→        latent_dim: int = 256,
    28→        dropout: float = 0.1,
    29→    ) -> None:
    30→        super().__init__()
    31→        self.input_dim = input_dim
    32→        self.latent_dim = latent_dim
    33→
    34→        # Encoder: input_dim → 512 → 512 → 384
    35→        self.encoder = nn.Sequential(
    36→            nn.Linear(input_dim, 512),
    37→            nn.LayerNorm(512),
    38→            nn.GELU(),
    39→            nn.Dropout(dropout),
    40→            nn.Linear(512, 512),
    41→            nn.LayerNorm(512),
    42→            nn.GELU(),
    43→            nn.Dropout(dropout),
    44→            nn.Linear(512, 384),
    45→            nn.LayerNorm(384),
    46→            nn.GELU(),
    47→        )
    48→
    49→        # Latent heads
    50→        self.mu_head = nn.Linear(384, latent_dim)
    51→        self.logvar_head = nn.Linear(384, latent_dim)
    52→
    53→        # Decoder: latent_dim → 384 → 512 → 512 → input_dim
    54→        self.decoder = nn.Sequential(
    55→            nn.Linear(latent_dim, 384),
    56→            nn.LayerNorm(384),
    57→            nn.GELU(),
    58→            nn.Dropout(dropout),
    59→            nn.Linear(384, 512),
    60→            nn.LayerNorm(512),
    61→            nn.GELU(),
    62→            nn.Dropout(dropout),
    63→            nn.Linear(512, 512),
    64→            nn.LayerNorm(512),
    65→            nn.GELU(),
    66→            nn.Linear(512, input_dim),
    67→        )
    68→
    69→    def encode(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    70→        """Encode input to (mu, logvar)."""
    71→        h = self.encoder(x)
    72→        return self.mu_head(h), self.logvar_head(h)
    73→
    74→    def decode(self, z: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    75→        """Decode latent vector to reconstruction."""
    76→        return self.decoder(z)
    77→
    78→    def reparameterize(self, mu: torch.Tensor, logvar: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    79→        """Sample z using reparameterization trick. In eval mode, return mu."""
    80→        if self.training:
    81→            std = torch.exp(0.5 * logvar)
    82→            eps = torch.randn_like(std)
    83→            return mu + eps * std
    84→        return mu
    85→
    86→    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    87→        """Full forward pass: encode → reparameterize → decode.
    88→
    89→        Returns (reconstruction, mu, logvar, z).
    90→        """
    91→        mu, logvar = self.encode(x)
    92→        z = self.reparameterize(mu, logvar)
    93→        recon = self.decode(z)
    94→        return recon, mu, logvar, z
    95→
    96→
    97→def flume_vae_loss(
    98→    x: torch.Tensor,
    99→    recon: torch.Tensor,
   100→    mu: torch.Tensor,
   101→    logvar: torch.Tensor,
   102→    *,
   103→    beta: float = 0.1,
   104→    free_bits: float = 0.125,
   105→    lambda_coherence: float = 0.01,
   106→) -> dict[str, torch.Tensor]:
   107→    """Compute FLUME VAE loss with KL annealing support and free-bits.
   108→
   109→    Returns dict with individual loss components and total.
   110→    """
   111→    # Reconstruction: MSE
   112→    recon_loss = F.mse_loss(recon, x, reduction="mean")
   113→
   114→    # KL divergence per dimension with free-bits
   115→    kl_per_dim = -0.5 * (1 + logvar - mu.pow(2) - logvar.exp())
   116→    # Free-bits: clamp minimum KL per dimension
   117→    if free_bits > 0:
   118→        kl_per_dim = torch.clamp(kl_per_dim, min=free_bits)
   119→    kl_loss = kl_per_dim.mean()
   120→
   121→    # HIHO coherence: per-sample mean(mu) should be near 0.5
   122→    sample_means = mu.mean(dim=1)  # (batch,)
   123→    coherence_loss = ((sample_means - 0.5) ** 2).mean()
   124→
   125→    total = recon_loss + beta * kl_loss + lambda_coherence * coherence_loss
   126→
   127→    return {
   128→        "total_loss": total,
   129→        "recon_loss": recon_loss,
   130→        "kl_loss": kl_loss,
   131→        "coherence_loss": coherence_loss,
   132→    }
   133→
```

```
     1→"""Training pipeline for FLUME VAE v2.
     2→
     3→Features:
     4→  - KL annealing (linear warmup)
     5→  - Free-bits per dimension
     6→  - Gradient clipping
     7→  - Checkpoint save/load
     8→  - Active unit monitoring
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→from pathlib import Path
    15→
    16→import torch
    17→import torch.nn as nn
    18→from torch.utils.data import DataLoader, TensorDataset
    19→
    20→from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE, flume_vae_loss
    21→
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→
    26→def kl_annealing_beta(step: int, warmup_steps: int, max_beta: float) -> float:
    27→    """Linear KL annealing: 0 → max_beta over warmup_steps."""
    28→    if warmup_steps <= 0:
    29→        return max_beta
    30→    return min(max_beta, max_beta * step / warmup_steps)
    31→
    32→
    33→def count_active_units(mu: torch.Tensor, threshold: float = 0.01) -> int:
    34→    """Count latent dimensions with variance above threshold across samples."""
    35→    var_per_dim = mu.var(dim=0)
    36→    return int((var_per_dim > threshold).sum().item())
    37→
    38→
    39→class VAETrainer:
    40→    """Train FLUME VAE with KL annealing and monitoring."""
    41→
    42→    def __init__(
    43→        self,
    44→        model: FlumeVAE,
    45→        lr: float = 1e-3,
    46→        weight_decay: float = 1e-4,
    47→        max_beta: float = 0.1,
    48→        warmup_fraction: float = 0.3,
    49→        free_bits: float = 0.125,
    50→        lambda_coherence: float = 0.01,
    51→        grad_clip: float = 1.0,
    52→    ) -> None:
    53→        self.model = model
    54→        self.max_beta = max_beta
    55→        self.warmup_fraction = warmup_fraction
    56→        self.free_bits = free_bits
    57→        self.lambda_coherence = lambda_coherence
    58→        self.grad_clip = grad_clip
    59→
    60→        self.optimizer = torch.optim.AdamW(model.parameters(), lr=lr, weight_decay=weight_decay)
    61→
    62→    def train(
    63→        self,
    64→        data: torch.Tensor,
    65→        epochs: int = 50,
    66→        batch_size: int = 64,
    67→    ) -> list[dict[str, float]]:
    68→        """Train VAE on embedded data. Returns per-epoch metrics."""
    69→        dataset = TensorDataset(data)
    70→        loader = DataLoader(dataset, batch_size=batch_size, shuffle=True)
    71→
    72→        total_steps = epochs * len(loader)
    73→        warmup_steps = int(total_steps * self.warmup_fraction)
    74→
    75→        history: list[dict[str, float]] = []
    76→        global_step = 0
    77→
    78→        self.model.train()
    79→        for epoch in range(epochs):
    80→            epoch_losses: dict[str, list[float]] = {
    81→                "total_loss": [],
    82→                "recon_loss": [],
    83→                "kl_loss": [],
    84→                "coherence_loss": [],
    85→            }
    86→            current_beta = 0.0
    87→
    88→            for (batch,) in loader:
    89→                current_beta = kl_annealing_beta(global_step, warmup_steps, self.max_beta)
    90→
    91→                recon, mu, logvar, _z = self.model(batch)
    92→                losses = flume_vae_loss(
    93→                    batch,
    94→                    recon,
    95→                    mu,
    96→                    logvar,
    97→                    beta=current_beta,
    98→                    free_bits=self.free_bits,
    99→                    lambda_coherence=self.lambda_coherence,
   100→                )
   101→
   102→                self.optimizer.zero_grad()
   103→                losses["total_loss"].backward()
   104→
   105→                if self.grad_clip > 0:
   106→                    nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(self.model.parameters(), self.grad_clip)
   107→
   108→                self.optimizer.step()
   109→                global_step += 1
   110→
   111→                for k in epoch_losses:
   112→                    epoch_losses[k].append(losses[k].item())
   113→
   114→            avg = {k: sum(v) / len(v) for k, v in epoch_losses.items()}
   115→            avg["beta"] = current_beta
   116→            avg["epoch"] = epoch
   117→            history.append(avg)
   118→
   119→            if (epoch + 1) % 10 == 0 or epoch == 0:
   120→                logger.info(
   121→                    "Epoch %d: loss=%.4f recon=%.4f kl=%.4f β=%.4f",
   122→                    epoch,
   123→                    avg["total_loss"],
   124→                    avg["recon_loss"],
   125→                    avg["kl_loss"],
   126→                    current_beta,
   127→                )
   128→
   129→        return history
   130→
   131→    def save_checkpoint(self, path: Path, epoch: int, metrics: dict | None = None) -> None:
   132→        """Save model + optimizer state."""
   133→        torch.save(
   134→            {
   135→                "model_state_dict": self.model.state_dict(),
   136→                "optimizer_state_dict": self.optimizer.state_dict(),
   137→                "epoch": epoch,
   138→                "metrics": metrics or {},
   139→                "config": {
   140→                    "input_dim": self.model.input_dim,
   141→                    "latent_dim": self.model.latent_dim,
   142→                    "max_beta": self.max_beta,
   143→                    "free_bits": self.free_bits,
   144→                },
   145→            },
   146→            path,
   147→        )
   148→        logger.info("Saved checkpoint to %s (epoch %d)", path, epoch)
   149→
   150→    def load_checkpoint(self, path: Path) -> dict:
   151→        """Load checkpoint into current model/optimizer."""
   152→        ckpt = torch.load(path, map_location="cpu", weights_only=True)
   153→        self.model.load_state_dict(ckpt["model_state_dict"])
   154→        self.optimizer.load_state_dict(ckpt["optimizer_state_dict"])
   155→        logger.info("Loaded checkpoint from %s (epoch %d)", path, ckpt["epoch"])
   156→        return {"epoch": ckpt["epoch"], "metrics": ckpt["metrics"], "config": ckpt["config"]}
   157→
```

     1→"""Training data pipeline for FLUME VAE v2.
     2→
     3→Generates synthetic task descriptions across 5 operation types,
     4→mines contrastive pairs from paraphrase groups, and embeds via
     5→an EmbeddingProvider.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→import itertools
    11→import logging
    12→import random
    13→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
    14→
    15→import numpy as np
    16→
    17→
    18→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    19→    from cohezion.flume.embedding_provider import EmbeddingProvider
    20→
    21→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    22→
    23→# Task templates organized by operation type and paraphrase group.
    24→# Each group contains semantically equivalent descriptions.
    25→_TASK_TEMPLATES: dict[str, list[list[str]]] = {
    26→    "generate": [
    27→        ["generate a summary report", "create a summary report", "produce summary report"],
    28→        ["write unit tests", "create unit tests", "generate test cases"],
    29→        ["generate API documentation", "create API docs", "produce API reference"],
    30→        ["build a data pipeline", "create data pipeline", "construct data pipeline"],
    31→        ["generate embeddings for text", "create text embeddings", "produce vector embeddings"],
    32→    ],
    33→    "analyze": [
    34→        ["analyze code quality", "assess code quality", "evaluate code quality"],
    35→        ["analyze performance bottlenecks", "identify performance issues", "find performance problems"],
    36→        ["review pull request changes", "analyze PR diff", "evaluate pull request"],
    37→        ["analyze test coverage", "assess test coverage", "evaluate test coverage gaps"],
    38→        ["analyze error patterns", "investigate error trends", "examine failure patterns"],
    39→    ],
    40→    "search": [
    41→        ["search for relevant documentation", "find related docs", "look up documentation"],
    42→        ["search codebase for pattern", "find code matching pattern", "grep for code pattern"],
    43→        ["search for similar issues", "find related bugs", "look up similar problems"],
    44→        ["search vault for decisions", "find past decisions", "query decision history"],
    45→        ["search for API endpoints", "find available endpoints", "discover API routes"],
    46→    ],
    47→    "transform": [
    48→        ["refactor the authentication module", "restructure auth module", "reorganize authentication code"],
    49→        ["transform data format to JSON", "convert data to JSON", "serialize data as JSON"],
    50→        ["migrate database schema", "update database schema", "transform DB schema"],
    51→        ["transform config to YAML", "convert configuration to YAML", "reformat config as YAML"],
    52→        ["normalize the input data", "standardize input format", "clean and normalize inputs"],
    53→    ],
    54→    "persist": [
    55→        ["save checkpoint to disk", "write checkpoint file", "persist model checkpoint"],
    56→        ["store results in database", "save results to DB", "persist results in storage"],
    57→        ["cache embeddings locally", "store embeddings in cache", "persist embedding vectors"],
    58→        ["log experiment results", "record experiment outcome", "persist experiment data"],
    59→        ["save session state", "persist session context", "store session snapshot"],
    60→    ],
    61→}
    62→
    63→
    64→class SyntheticTaskGenerator:
    65→    """Generate synthetic task descriptions from templates."""
    66→
    67→    def __init__(self, seed: int | None = None) -> None:
    68→        self._rng = random.Random(seed)
    69→
    70→    def generate(self, n: int = 5000) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
    71→        """Generate n task descriptions with operation types and group IDs."""
    72→        # Flatten templates into (op_type, group_id, text) triples
    73→        all_items: list[tuple[str, str, str]] = []
    74→        for op_type, groups in _TASK_TEMPLATES.items():
    75→            for g_idx, group in enumerate(groups):
    76→                group_id = f"{op_type}_{g_idx}"
    77→                for text in group:
    78→                    all_items.append((op_type, group_id, text))
    79→
    80→        tasks: list[dict[str, str]] = []
    81→        while len(tasks) < n:
    82→            item = self._rng.choice(all_items)
    83→            tasks.append(
    84→                {
    85→                    "text": item[2],
    86→                    "op_type": item[0],
    87→                    "group_id": item[1],
    88→                }
    89→            )
    90→
    91→        return tasks[:n]
    92→
    93→
    94→class ContrastivePairMiner:
    95→    """Mine positive (same-group) pairs for contrastive learning."""
    96→
    97→    def mine_pairs(self, tasks: list[dict]) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
    98→        """Return (anchor_idx, positive_idx) pairs from same group."""
    99→        groups: dict[str, list[int]] = {}
   100→        for idx, t in enumerate(tasks):
   101→            gid = t["group_id"]
   102→            groups.setdefault(gid, []).append(idx)
   103→
   104→        pairs: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
   105→        for indices in groups.values():
   106→            if len(indices) >= 2:
   107→                for a, b in itertools.combinations(indices, 2):
   108→                    pairs.append((a, b))
   109→
   110→        return pairs
   111→
   112→
   113→class TrainingDataPipeline:
   114→    """Full data preparation: generate tasks → embed → mine pairs."""
   115→
   116→    def __init__(self, embedding_provider: EmbeddingProvider, seed: int | None = None) -> None:
   117→        self._provider = embedding_provider
   118→        self._task_gen = SyntheticTaskGenerator(seed=seed)
   119→        self._pair_miner = ContrastivePairMiner()
   120→
   121→    def prepare(self, n_synthetic: int = 5000, batch_size: int = 50) -> dict:
   122→        """Generate tasks, embed them, and mine contrastive pairs.
   123→
   124→        Returns dict with 'embeddings' (N x dim), 'texts', 'tasks', 'pairs'.
   125→        """
   126→        tasks = self._task_gen.generate(n=n_synthetic)
   127→        texts = [t["text"] for t in tasks]
   128→
   129→        # Batch embed
   130→        all_embeddings: list[np.ndarray] = []
   131→        for i in range(0, len(texts), batch_size):
   132→            batch = texts[i : i + batch_size]
   133→            emb = self._provider.embed_batch(batch)
   134→            all_embeddings.append(emb)
   135→
   136→        embeddings = np.concatenate(all_embeddings, axis=0)
   137→        pairs = self._pair_miner.mine_pairs(tasks)
   138→
   139→        logger.info(
   140→            "Prepared %d samples (%dD), %d contrastive pairs",
   141→            len(texts),
   142→            embeddings.shape[1],
   143→            len(pairs),
   144→        )
   145→
   146→        return {
   147→            "embeddings": embeddings,
   148→            "texts": texts,
   149→            "tasks": tasks,
   150→            "pairs": pairs,
   151→        }
   152→
```

     1→"""Evaluation framework for FLUME VAE v2.
     2→
     3→Metrics for all red flags:
     4→  1. KL health (collapse detection, active units)
     5→  2. Reconstruction fidelity (cosine similarity)
     6→  3. Paraphrase discrimination (Precision@1)
     7→  4. Similarity preservation (Spearman rho)
     8→"""
     9→
    10→from __future__ import annotations
    11→
    12→import logging
    13→
    14→import numpy as np
    15→import torch
    16→from scipy import stats
    17→
    18→from cohezion.flume.train_vae import count_active_units
    19→from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE, flume_vae_loss
    20→
    21→
    22→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    23→
    24→
    25→def reconstruction_cosine_similarity(original: np.ndarray, reconstructed: np.ndarray) -> float:
    26→    """Mean cosine similarity between original and reconstructed embeddings."""
    27→    # Normalize
    28→    orig_norm = original / (np.linalg.norm(original, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-8)
    29→    recon_norm = reconstructed / (np.linalg.norm(reconstructed, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-8)
    30→    # Per-sample cosine similarity
    31→    cos_sims = (orig_norm * recon_norm).sum(axis=1)
    32→    return float(cos_sims.mean())
    33→
    34→
    35→def paraphrase_precision_at_1(embeddings: np.ndarray, pairs: list[tuple[int, int]]) -> float:
    36→    """Precision@1: for each anchor, is the paraphrase the nearest neighbor?"""
    37→    if not pairs:
    38→        return 0.0
    39→
    40→    # Normalize embeddings
    41→    norms = np.linalg.norm(embeddings, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-8
    42→    normed = embeddings / norms
    43→
    44→    # Cosine similarity matrix
    45→    sim_matrix = normed @ normed.T
    46→    # Zero out self-similarity
    47→    np.fill_diagonal(sim_matrix, -np.inf)
    48→
    49→    hits = 0
    50→    total = 0
    51→    for anchor, positive in pairs:
    52→        nn = int(np.argmax(sim_matrix[anchor]))
    53→        if nn == positive:
    54→            hits += 1
    55→        total += 1
    56→
    57→    return float(hits / total) if total > 0 else 0.0
    58→
    59→
    60→def kl_health_check(
    61→    kl_value: float,
    62→    active_units: int,
    63→    total_units: int,
    64→    kl_threshold: float = 0.1,
    65→    active_threshold: int = 128,
    66→) -> dict:
    67→    """Check KL divergence health (Red Flag 1)."""
    68→    reasons = []
    69→    if kl_value < kl_threshold:
    70→        reasons.append(f"KL too low: {kl_value:.4f} < {kl_threshold}")
    71→    if active_units < active_threshold:
    72→        reasons.append(f"Active units too low: {active_units}/{total_units} < {active_threshold}")
    73→
    74→    return {
    75→        "healthy": len(reasons) == 0,
    76→        "kl_value": kl_value,
    77→        "active_units": active_units,
    78→        "total_units": total_units,
    79→        "reason": "; ".join(reasons) if reasons else "OK",
    80→    }
    81→
    82→
    83→def similarity_preservation_spearman(original: np.ndarray, latent: np.ndarray) -> float:
    84→    """Spearman rank correlation between pairwise similarities in original and latent space."""
    85→    n = original.shape[0]
    86→    if n < 3:
    87→        return 0.0
    88→
    89→    # Normalize
    90→    orig_norm = original / (np.linalg.norm(original, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-8)
    91→    lat_norm = latent / (np.linalg.norm(latent, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-8)
    92→
    93→    # Pairwise cosine similarities (upper triangle)
    94→    orig_sims = orig_norm @ orig_norm.T
    95→    lat_sims = lat_norm @ lat_norm.T
    96→
    97→    # Extract upper triangle
    98→    triu_idx = np.triu_indices(n, k=1)
    99→    orig_flat = orig_sims[triu_idx]
   100→    lat_flat = lat_sims[triu_idx]
   101→
   102→    rho, _ = stats.spearmanr(orig_flat, lat_flat)
   103→    return float(rho)
   104→
   105→
   106→class VAEEvaluator:
   107→    """Run full evaluation suite on trained FLUME VAE."""
   108→
   109→    def __init__(self, model: FlumeVAE) -> None:
   110→        self.model = model
   111→
   112→    @torch.no_grad()
   113→    def evaluate(
   114→        self,
   115→        data: torch.Tensor,
   116→        pairs: list[tuple[int, int]],
   117→    ) -> dict:
   118→        """Run all red-flag evaluations. Returns metrics dict."""
   119→        self.model.eval()
   120→
   121→        # Forward pass
   122→        recon, mu, logvar, _z = self.model(data)
   123→
   124→        # Reconstruction fidelity (Red Flag 2)
   125→        orig_np = data.numpy()
   126→        recon_np = recon.numpy()
   127→        recon_sim = reconstruction_cosine_similarity(orig_np, recon_np)
   128→
   129→        # KL health (Red Flag 1)
   130→        losses = flume_vae_loss(data, recon, mu, logvar, beta=1.0, free_bits=0.0)
   131→        kl_value = losses["kl_loss"].item()
   132→        active = count_active_units(mu)
   133→        kl_check = kl_health_check(kl_value, active, mu.shape[1])
   134→
   135→        # Paraphrase discrimination (Red Flag 3)
   136→        # Use mu (latent mean) as embedding
   137→        mu_np = mu.numpy()
   138→        p_at_1 = paraphrase_precision_at_1(mu_np, pairs)
   139→
   140→        # Similarity preservation (Red Flag 4)
   141→        spearman = similarity_preservation_spearman(orig_np, mu_np)
   142→
   143→        results = {
   144→            "reconstruction_cosine_sim": recon_sim,
   145→            "kl_health": kl_check,
   146→            "kl_value": kl_value,
   147→            "active_units": active,
   148→            "paraphrase_p_at_1": p_at_1,
   149→            "similarity_spearman": spearman,
   150→        }
   151→
   152→        logger.info("Evaluation results: %s", {k: v for k, v in results.items() if k != "kl_health"})
   153→
   154→        return results
   155→
```

     1→"""Embedding providers for FLUME VAE input.
     2→
     3→Three providers with fallback chain:
     4→  1. OllamaEmbeddingProvider — 768D via nomic-embed-text (best quality)
     5→  2. HashFallbackProvider — 256D via SHA-256 (always available, no semantics)
     6→  3. CachedEmbeddingProvider — LRU cache wrapper around any provider
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import hashlib
    12→import logging
    13→from collections import OrderedDict
    14→from typing import Protocol
    15→
    16→import numpy as np
    17→import requests
    18→
    19→
    20→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    21→
    22→
    23→class EmbeddingProvider(Protocol):
    24→    """Protocol for embedding providers."""
    25→
    26→    @property
    27→    def embedding_dim(self) -> int: ...
    28→
    29→    def embed(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray: ...
    30→
    31→    def embed_batch(self, texts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray: ...
    32→
    33→
    34→class OllamaEmbeddingProvider:
    35→    """Embed text via Ollama nomic-embed-text (768D)."""
    36→
    37→    def __init__(
    38→        self,
    39→        model: str = "nomic-embed-text",
    40→        base_url: str = "http://localhost:11434",
    41→        timeout: float = 30.0,
    42→    ) -> None:
    43→        self.model = model
    44→        self.base_url = base_url.rstrip("/")
    45→        self.timeout = timeout
    46→
    47→    @property
    48→    def embedding_dim(self) -> int:
    49→        return 768
    50→
    51→    def embed(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:
    52→        """Embed single text to 768D normalized vector."""
    53→        try:
    54→            resp = requests.post(
    55→                f"{self.base_url}/api/embed",
    56→                json={"model": self.model, "input": text},
    57→                timeout=self.timeout,
    58→            )
    59→            resp.raise_for_status()
    60→            data = resp.json()
    61→            vec = np.array(data["embeddings"][0], dtype=np.float32)
    62→            norm = np.linalg.norm(vec)
    63→            if norm > 0:
    64→                vec /= norm
    65→            return vec
    66→        except Exception as e:
    67→            raise ConnectionError(f"Ollama embedding failed: {e}") from e
    68→
    69→    def embed_batch(self, texts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray:
    70→        """Embed batch of texts. Returns (N, 768) array."""
    71→        try:
    72→            resp = requests.post(
    73→                f"{self.base_url}/api/embed",
    74→                json={"model": self.model, "input": texts},
    75→                timeout=self.timeout,
    76→            )
    77→            resp.raise_for_status()
    78→            data = resp.json()
    79→            vecs = np.array(data["embeddings"], dtype=np.float32)
    80→            norms = np.linalg.norm(vecs, axis=1, keepdims=True)
    81→            norms = np.where(norms > 0, norms, 1.0)
    82→            return vecs / norms
    83→        except Exception as e:
    84→            raise ConnectionError(f"Ollama batch embedding failed: {e}") from e
    85→
    86→
    87→class HashFallbackProvider:
    88→    """Deterministic SHA-256 hash embedding (256D). No semantic meaning."""
    89→
    90→    @property
    91→    def embedding_dim(self) -> int:
    92→        return 256
    93→
    94→    def embed(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:
    95→        """Embed text to 256D via SHA-256 hash expansion."""
    96→        hash_bytes = hashlib.sha256(text.encode()).digest()
    97→        vec = np.zeros(256, dtype=np.float32)
    98→        for i in range(256):
    99→            vec[i] = hash_bytes[i % len(hash_bytes)] / 255.0
   100→        norm = np.linalg.norm(vec)
   101→        if norm > 0:
   102→            vec /= norm
   103→        return vec
   104→
   105→    def embed_batch(self, texts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray:
   106→        """Embed batch of texts. Returns (N, 256) array."""
   107→        return np.stack([self.embed(t) for t in texts])
   108→
   109→
   110→class CachedEmbeddingProvider:
   111→    """LRU cache wrapper around any EmbeddingProvider."""
   112→
   113→    def __init__(self, inner: EmbeddingProvider, max_size: int = 10_000) -> None:
   114→        self._inner = inner
   115→        self._max_size = max_size
   116→        self._cache: OrderedDict[str, np.ndarray] = OrderedDict()
   117→
   118→    @property
   119→    def embedding_dim(self) -> int:
   120→        return self._inner.embedding_dim
   121→
   122→    def embed(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:
   123→        if text in self._cache:
   124→            self._cache.move_to_end(text)
   125→            return self._cache[text]
   126→        result = self._inner.embed(text)
   127→        self._cache[text] = result
   128→        if len(self._cache) > self._max_size:
   129→            self._cache.popitem(last=False)
   130→        return result
   131→
   132→    def embed_batch(self, texts: list[str]) -> np.ndarray:
   133→        return np.stack([self.embed(t) for t in texts])
   134→
   135→
   136→def create_embedding_provider(
   137→    *,
   138→    use_cache: bool = True,
   139→    cache_size: int = 10_000,
   140→    require_ollama: bool = False,
   141→) -> EmbeddingProvider:
   142→    """Create the best available embedding provider.
   143→
   144→    Tries Ollama first, falls back to hash if unavailable.
   145→    """
   146→    provider: EmbeddingProvider
   147→    if require_ollama:
   148→        provider = OllamaEmbeddingProvider()
   149→    else:
   150→        try:
   151→            ollama = OllamaEmbeddingProvider()
   152→            # Quick probe to check availability
   153→            ollama.embed("test")
   154→            provider = ollama
   155→            logger.info("Using Ollama nomic-embed-text (768D)")
   156→        except Exception:
   157→            provider = HashFallbackProvider()
   158→            logger.info("Ollama unavailable, using hash fallback (256D)")
   159→
   160→    if use_cache:
   161→        provider = CachedEmbeddingProvider(provider, max_size=cache_size)
   162→
   163→    return provider
   164→
```

     1→"""FLUME VAE Encoder for production semantic embeddings.
     2→
     3→Supports two checkpoint formats:
     4→  v1: SimpleEncoder (256→512→mu) with SHA-256 hash input (legacy)
     5→  v2: FlumeVAE (768→...→256) with Ollama nomic-embed-text input (new)
     6→
     7→Falls back to deterministic hash encoding if no model or Ollama available.
     8→"""
     9→
    10→import hashlib
    11→import logging
    12→from pathlib import Path
    13→
    14→import numpy as np
    15→
    16→from cohezion.flume.embedding_provider import OllamaEmbeddingProvider
    17→
    18→
    19→try:
    20→    import torch
    21→    import torch.nn as nn
    22→
    23→    TORCH_AVAILABLE = True
    24→except ImportError:
    25→    TORCH_AVAILABLE = False
    26→
    27→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    28→
    29→
    30→class SimpleEncoder(nn.Module):
    31→    """Legacy v1 encoder: Linear(256->512) + ReLU + Linear(512->512)."""
    32→
    33→    def __init__(self, input_size: int = 256, hidden_size: int = 512):
    34→        super().__init__()
    35→        self.encoder = nn.Sequential(
    36→            nn.Linear(input_size, hidden_size),
    37→            nn.ReLU(),
    38→            nn.Linear(hidden_size, hidden_size),
    39→        )
    40→
    41→    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    42→        return self.encoder(x)
    43→
    44→
    45→class FlumeVAEEncoder:
    46→    """Production VAE encoder for semantic embeddings."""
    47→
    48→    DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH = Path("./data/flume/checkpoints/flume_vae_ep50.pt")
    49→    EMBEDDING_DIM = 256
    50→
    51→    def __init__(
    52→        self,
    53→        model_path: Path | None = None,
    54→        device: str = "cpu",
    55→        fallback_to_hash: bool = True,
    56→    ):
    57→        self.device = device
    58→        self.model_path = model_path or self.DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH
    59→        self.fallback_to_hash = fallback_to_hash
    60→        self.encoder = None
    61→        self.mu_head = None
    62→        self._vae_v2 = None
    63→        self._ollama_provider = None
    64→        self.enabled = False
    65→        self._version = 0
    66→
    67→        if TORCH_AVAILABLE:
    68→            self._load_encoder()
    69→        elif not fallback_to_hash:
    70→            logger.warning("PyTorch not available and fallback disabled")
    71→
    72→    def _load_encoder(self) -> None:
    73→        """Load encoder from checkpoint (v1 or v2 format)."""
    74→        try:
    75→            if not self.model_path.exists():
    76→                logger.warning("Model path not found: %s", self.model_path)
    77→                return
    78→
    79→            checkpoint = torch.load(self.model_path, map_location=self.device, weights_only=True)
    80→
    81→            # Detect v2 checkpoint (has model_state_dict and version or config with input_dim)
    82→            if "model_state_dict" in checkpoint:
    83→                self._load_v2(checkpoint)
    84→            elif "encoder" in checkpoint and "mu_head" in checkpoint:
    85→                self._load_v1(checkpoint)
    86→            else:
    87→                logger.warning("Unknown checkpoint format")
    88→
    89→        except Exception as e:
    90→            logger.warning("Failed to load VAE encoder: %s", e)
    91→            self.encoder = None
    92→            self.mu_head = None
    93→            self._vae_v2 = None
    94→
    95→    def _load_v2(self, checkpoint: dict) -> None:
    96→        """Load v2 FlumeVAE model."""
    97→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
    98→
    99→        config = checkpoint.get("config", {})
   100→        input_dim = config.get("input_dim", 768)
   101→        latent_dim = config.get("latent_dim", 256)
   102→
   103→        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=input_dim, latent_dim=latent_dim)
   104→        model.load_state_dict(checkpoint["model_state_dict"])
   105→        model.to(self.device)
   106→        model.eval()
   107→        self._vae_v2 = model
   108→        self._version = 2
   109→
   110→        # Try to set up Ollama provider for semantic input
   111→        try:
   112→            self._ollama_provider = OllamaEmbeddingProvider()
   113→            # Quick probe
   114→            self._ollama_provider.embed("test")
   115→            logger.info("Loaded FLUME VAE v2 with Ollama embeddings")
   116→        except Exception:
   117→            self._ollama_provider = None
   118→            logger.info("Loaded FLUME VAE v2 (Ollama unavailable, hash fallback)")
   119→
   120→        self.enabled = True
   121→
   122→    def _load_v1(self, checkpoint: dict) -> None:
   123→        """Load legacy v1 encoder."""
   124→        self.encoder = SimpleEncoder(input_size=256, hidden_size=512)
   125→        self.encoder.encoder.load_state_dict(checkpoint["encoder"])
   126→        self.encoder.to(self.device)
   127→        self.encoder.eval()
   128→
   129→        self.mu_head = nn.Linear(512, self.EMBEDDING_DIM)
   130→        self.mu_head.load_state_dict(checkpoint["mu_head"])
   131→        self.mu_head.to(self.device)
   132→        self.mu_head.eval()
   133→
   134→        self._version = 1
   135→        self.enabled = True
   136→        logger.info("Loaded FLUME VAE v1 from checkpoint")
   137→
   138→    def encode(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:
   139→        """Encode text to 256D semantic embedding."""
   140→        if self.enabled:
   141→            if self._version == 2 and self._vae_v2 is not None:
   142→                return self._vae_v2_encode(text)
   143→            if self._version == 1 and self.encoder is not None:
   144→                return self._vae_v1_encode(text)
   145→        if self.fallback_to_hash:
   146→            return self._hash_encode(text)
   147→        raise RuntimeError("VAE encoder not available and fallback disabled")
   148→
   149→    def _vae_v2_encode(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:
   150→        """Encode using v2 model: text → Ollama 768D → VAE → 256D."""
   151→        try:
   152→            with torch.no_grad():
   153→                if self._ollama_provider is not None:
   154→                    input_vec = self._ollama_provider.embed(text)
   155→                else:
   156→                    # Fallback: hash to 768D (poor quality but functional)
   157→                    input_vec = self._hash_encode_nd(text, 768)
   158→
   159→                tensor = torch.from_numpy(input_vec).float().unsqueeze(0).to(self.device)
   160→                mu, _ = self._vae_v2.encode(tensor)
   161→                embedding = mu.squeeze(0).cpu().numpy().astype(np.float32)
   162→                norm = np.linalg.norm(embedding)
   163→                if norm > 0:
   164→                    embedding /= norm
   165→                return embedding
   166→        except Exception as e:
   167→            logger.debug("VAE v2 encoding failed: %s, using hash fallback", e)
   168→            return self._hash_encode(text)
   169→
   170→    def _vae_v1_encode(self, text: str) -> np.ndarray:
   171→        """Encode using legacy v1 model: text → hash 256D → encoder → mu → 256D."""
   172→        try:
   173→            with torch.no_grad():
   174→                hash_embedding = self._hash_encode(text)
   175→                hash_tensor = torch.from_numpy(hash_embedding).float().unsqueeze(0).to(self.device)
   176→                encoded = self.encoder(hash_tensor)
   177→                latent = self.mu_head(encoded)
   178→                embedding = latent.squeeze(0).cpu().numpy().astype(np.float32)
   179→                norm = np.linalg.norm(embedding)
   180→                if norm > 0:
   181→                    embedding /= norm
   182→                return embedding
   183→        except Exception as e:
   184→            logger.debug("VAE v1 encoding failed: %s, using hash fallback", e)
   185→            return self._hash_encode(text)
   186→
   187→    @staticmethod
   188→    def _hash_encode(text: str) -> np.ndarray:
   189→        """256D normalized embedding from SHA-256 hash."""
   190→        hash_bytes = hashlib.sha256(text.encode()).digest()
   191→        embedding = np.zeros(256, dtype=np.float32)
   192→        for i in range(256):
   193→            embedding[i] = hash_bytes[i % len(hash_bytes)] / 255.0
   194→        norm = np.linalg.norm(embedding)
   195→        if norm > 0:
   196→            embedding /= norm
   197→        return embedding
   198→
   199→    @staticmethod
   200→    def _hash_encode_nd(text: str, dim: int) -> np.ndarray:
   201→        """N-dimensional normalized embedding from SHA-256 hash."""
   202→        hash_bytes = hashlib.sha256(text.encode()).digest()
   203→        embedding = np.zeros(dim, dtype=np.float32)
   204→        for i in range(dim):
   205→            embedding[i] = hash_bytes[i % len(hash_bytes)] / 255.0
   206→        norm = np.linalg.norm(embedding)
   207→        if norm > 0:
   208→            embedding /= norm
   209→        return embedding
   210→
   211→    def is_available(self) -> bool:
   212→        return self.enabled
   213→
   214→
   215→# Global encoder instance
   216→_encoder_instance: FlumeVAEEncoder | None = None
   217→
   218→
   219→def get_encoder() -> FlumeVAEEncoder:
   220→    """Get or create singleton VAE encoder."""
   221→    global _encoder_instance
   222→    if _encoder_instance is None:
   223→        _encoder_instance = FlumeVAEEncoder()
   224→    return _encoder_instance
   225→
   226→
   227→def reset_encoder() -> None:
   228→    """Reset encoder instance (for testing)."""
   229→    global _encoder_instance
   230→    _encoder_instance = None
   231→

     1→"""Tests for FLUME VAE evaluation framework."""

    10→class TestReconstructionMetric:
    11→    """Test cosine similarity reconstruction metric."""
    12→
    13→    def test_perfect_reconstruction(self):
    14→        """cos_sim should be 1.0 for identical inputs."""
    15→        from cohezion.flume.evaluate_vae import reconstruction_cosine_similarity
    16→
    17→        x = np.random.randn(10, 768).astype(np.float32)
    18→        score = reconstruction_cosine_similarity(x, x)
    19→        assert abs(score - 1.0) < 1e-5
    20→
    21→    def test_random_reconstruction_below_one(self):
    22→        """cos_sim should be < 1.0 for non-identical vectors."""
    23→        from cohezion.flume.evaluate_vae import reconstruction_cosine_similarity
    24→
    25→        x = np.random.randn(10, 768).astype(np.float32)
    26→        recon = np.random.randn(10, 768).astype(np.float32)
    27→        score = reconstruction_cosine_similarity(x, recon)
    28→        assert score < 0.99

    33→
    34→        x = np.random.randn(5, 768).astype(np.float32)
    35→        score = reconstruction_cosine_similarity(x, x)

     1→"""Tests for FLUME VAE v2 — encoder, decoder, reparameterization, loss."""

     4→
     5→import numpy as np

     9→
    10→class TestFlumeVAEEncoder:
    11→    """Test VAE encoder produces correct output shapes."""
    12→
    13→    def test_encoder_output_shape(self):
    14→        """Encoder should map 768D input to mu(256) and logvar(256)."""
    15→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
    16→
    17→        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)
    18→        x = torch.randn(8, 768)
    19→        mu, logvar = model.encode(x)
    20→
    21→        assert mu.shape == (8, 256)
    22→        assert logvar.shape == (8, 256)
    23→
    24→    def test_encoder_mu_is_finite(self):
    25→        """Encoder output should not contain NaN or Inf."""
    26→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
    27→
    28→        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)
    29→        x = torch.randn(4, 768)
    30→        mu, logvar = model.encode(x)
    31→
    32→        assert torch.isfinite(mu).all()
    33→        assert torch.isfinite(logvar).all()
    34→
    35→
    36→class TestFlumeVAEDecoder:
    37→    """Test VAE decoder produces correct output shapes."""
    38→
    39→    def test_decoder_output_shape(self):
    40→        """Decoder should map 256D latent to 768D reconstruction."""
    41→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
    42→
    43→        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)
    44→        z = torch.randn(8, 256)
    45→        recon = model.decode(z)
    46→
    47→        assert recon.shape == (8, 768)
    48→
    49→    def test_decoder_output_is_finite(self):
    50→        """Decoder output should not contain NaN or Inf."""
    51→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
    52→
    53→        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)
    54→        z = torch.randn(4, 256)
    55→        recon = model.decode(z)
    56→
    57→        assert torch.isfinite(recon).all()
    58→
    59→
    60→class TestReparameterization:
    61→    """Test the reparameterization trick."""
    62→
    63→    def test_reparameterize_produces_different_z_each_call(self):
    64→        """Two calls with same mu/logvar should produce different z (stochastic)."""
    65→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
    66→
    67→        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)
    68→        model.train()
    69→        mu = torch.zeros(4, 256)
    70→        logvar = torch.zeros(4, 256)
    71→
    72→        z1 = model.reparameterize(mu, logvar)
    73→        z2 = model.reparameterize(mu, logvar)
    74→
    75→        # With std=1 (logvar=0), samples should differ
    76→        assert not torch.allclose(z1, z2, atol=1e-6)
    77→
    78→    def test_reparameterize_eval_mode_returns_mu(self):
    79→        """In eval mode, reparameterize should return mu directly."""
    80→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
    81→
    82→        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)
    83→        model.eval()
    84→        mu = torch.randn(4, 256)
    85→        logvar = torch.full((4, 256), -2.0)
    86→
    87→        z = model.reparameterize(mu, logvar)
    88→        torch.testing.assert_close(z, mu)
    89→

    91→class TestForwardPass:
    92→    """Test full forward pass."""
    93→
    94→    def test_forward_returns_all_components(self):
    95→        """Forward should return (recon, mu, logvar, z)."""
    96→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
    97→
    98→        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)
    99→        x = torch.randn(8, 768)
   100→        recon, mu, logvar, z = model(x)
   101→
   102→        assert recon.shape == (8, 768)
   103→        assert mu.shape == (8, 256)
   104→        assert logvar.shape == (8, 256)
   105→        assert z.shape == (8, 256)
   106→
   107→    def test_forward_reconstruction_is_finite(self):
   108→        """Full forward pass should produce finite outputs."""
   109→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
   110→
   111→        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)
   112→        x = torch.randn(4, 768)
   113→        recon, mu, logvar, z = model(x)
   114→
   115→        assert torch.isfinite(recon).all()
   116→        assert torch.isfinite(mu).all()
   117→        assert torch.isfinite(logvar).all()
   118→        assert torch.isfinite(z).all()

   121→class TestLossFunction:
   122→    """Test VAE loss computation."""
   123→
   124→    def test_reconstruction_loss_is_positive(self):
   125→        """MSE reconstruction loss should be positive."""
   126→        from cohezion.flume.vae import flume_vae_loss
   127→
   128→        x = torch.randn(8, 768)
   129→        recon = torch.randn(8, 768)
   130→        mu = torch.randn(8, 256)
   131→        logvar = torch.zeros(8, 256)
   132→
   133→        loss_dict = flume_vae_loss(x, recon, mu, logvar, beta=0.1)
   134→
   135→        assert loss_dict["recon_loss"].item() > 0
   136→        assert loss_dict["total_loss"].item() > 0
   137→
   138→    def test_kl_loss_is_positive(self):
   139→        """KL divergence should be positive for non-standard normal."""
   140→        from cohezion.flume.vae import flume_vae_loss
   141→
   142→        x = torch.randn(8, 768)
   143→        recon = x.clone()  # Perfect reconstruction
   144→        mu = torch.ones(8, 256) * 2.0  # Non-zero mean
   145→        logvar = torch.ones(8, 256)  # Non-unit variance
   146→
   147→        loss_dict = flume_vae_loss(x, recon, mu, logvar, beta=1.0)
   148→
   149→        assert loss_dict["kl_loss"].item() > 0
   150→
   151→    def test_kl_loss_zero_for_standard_normal(self):
   152→        """KL should be near zero when mu=0, logvar=0 (standard normal)."""
   153→        from cohezion.flume.vae import flume_vae_loss
   154→
   155→        x = torch.randn(8, 768)
   156→        mu = torch.zeros(8, 256)
   157→        logvar = torch.zeros(8, 256)
   158→
   159→        loss_dict = flume_vae_loss(x, x.clone(), mu, logvar, beta=1.0, free_bits=0.0)
   160→
   161→        assert loss_dict["kl_loss"].item() < 0.01
   162→
   163→    def test_free_bits_clamps_kl(self):
   164→        """Free-bits should enforce minimum KL per dimension."""
   165→        from cohezion.flume.vae import flume_vae_loss
   166→
   167→        x = torch.randn(8, 768)
   168→        mu = torch.zeros(8, 256)
   169→        logvar = torch.zeros(8, 256)
   170→
   171→        # With free_bits=0, KL ~0 for standard normal
   172→        loss_no_fb = flume_vae_loss(x, x, mu, logvar, beta=1.0, free_bits=0.0)
   173→        # With free_bits=0.125, KL should be >= 0.125 * 256
   174→        loss_fb = flume_vae_loss(x, x, mu, logvar, beta=1.0, free_bits=0.125)
   175→
   176→        assert loss_fb["kl_loss"].item() >= loss_no_fb["kl_loss"].item()
   177→
   178→    def test_beta_scales_kl_contribution(self):
   179→        """Higher beta should increase KL contribution to total loss."""
   180→        from cohezion.flume.vae import flume_vae_loss
   181→
   182→        x = torch.randn(8, 768)
   183→        recon = torch.randn(8, 768)
   184→        mu = torch.ones(8, 256)
   185→        logvar = torch.zeros(8, 256)
   186→
   187→        loss_low = flume_vae_loss(x, recon, mu, logvar, beta=0.01)
   188→        loss_high = flume_vae_loss(x, recon, mu, logvar, beta=1.0)
   189→
   190→        assert loss_high["total_loss"].item() > loss_low["total_loss"].item()
   191→
   192→    def test_coherence_loss_penalizes_deviation_from_hiho(self):
   193→        """Coherence loss should be higher when mean(mu) deviates from 0.5."""
   194→        from cohezion.flume.vae import flume_vae_loss
   195→
   196→        x = torch.randn(8, 768)
   197→        mu_centered = torch.full((8, 256), 0.5)
   198→        mu_off = torch.full((8, 256), 2.0)
   199→        logvar = torch.zeros(8, 256)
   200→
   201→        loss_centered = flume_vae_loss(x, x, mu_centered, logvar, lambda_coherence=1.0)
   202→        loss_off = flume_vae_loss(x, x, mu_off, logvar, lambda_coherence=1.0)
   203→
   204→        assert loss_off["coherence_loss"].item() > loss_centered["coherence_loss"].item()
   205→

   207→class TestModelConfig:
   208→    """Test model parameter counts and configuration."""
   209→
   210→    def test_parameter_count_reasonable(self):
   211→        """Model should have ~2M parameters (not too large for CPU training)."""
   212→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
   213→
   214→        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)
   215→        total_params = sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters())
   216→
   217→        # Should be between 1M and 4M params
   218→        assert 1_000_000 < total_params < 4_000_000, f"Got {total_params} params"
   219→
   220→    def test_different_latent_dims(self):
   221→        """Model should work with different latent dimensions."""
   222→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
   223→
   224→        for latent_dim in [64, 128, 256]:
   225→            model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=latent_dim)
   226→            x = torch.randn(2, 768)
   227→            recon, mu, logvar, z = model(x)
   228→            assert mu.shape == (2, latent_dim)
   229→            assert recon.shape == (2, 768)

     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch
     7→
     8→import numpy as np
     9→import pytest
    10→import torch
    11→
    12→
    13→class TestVAEEncoderV2Integration:
    14→    """Test vae_encoder.py with new VAE model."""
    15→
    16→    def test_new_vae_produces_256d_output(self, tmp_path: Path):
    17→        """New VAE model loaded via vae_encoder should produce 256D."""
    18→        from cohezion.flume.vae import FlumeVAE
    19→        from cohezion.flume.vae_encoder import FlumeVAEEncoder
    20→
    21→        # Create and save a v2 model checkpoint
    22→        model = FlumeVAE(input_dim=768, latent_dim=256)
    23→        ckpt_path = tmp_path / "test_v2.pt"
    24→        torch.save(
    25→            {
    26→                "model_state_dict": model.state_dict(),
    27→                "config": {"input_dim": 768, "latent_dim": 256},
    28→                "version": 2,
    29→            },
    30→            ckpt_path,
    31→        )
    32→
    33→        # Mock Ollama to return 768D
    34→        fake_768d = np.random.randn(768).astype(np.float32)
    35→        fake_768d /= np.linalg.norm(fake_768d)
    36→
    37→        with patch("cohezion.flume.vae_encoder.OllamaEmbeddingProvider") as MockOllama:
    38→            MockOllama.return_value.embed.return_value = fake_768d
    39→            encoder = FlumeVAEEncoder(model_path=ckpt_path, fallback_to_hash=True)
    40→
    41→        if encoder.is_available():
    42→            result = encoder.encode("deploy the API")
    43→            assert result.shape == (256,)
    44→            assert abs(np.linalg.norm(result) - 1.0) < 1e-4
    45→
    46→    def test_fallback_chain_works_without_ollama(self):
    47→        """When Ollama is unavailable and no v2 checkpoint, should hash fallback."""
    48→        from cohezion.flume.vae_encoder import FlumeVAEEncoder
    49→
    50→        encoder = FlumeVAEEncoder(
    51→            model_path=Path("/nonexistent/model.pt"),
    52→            fallback_to_hash=True,
    53→        )
    54→        result = encoder.encode("deploy the API")
    55→        assert result.shape == (256,)
    56→        assert abs(np.linalg.norm(result) - 1.0) < 1e-4
    57→
    58→    def test_existing_tests_still_pass(self):
    59→        """Existing public interface should be unchanged."""
    60→        from cohezion.flume.vae_encoder import FlumeVAEEncoder
    61→
    62→        encoder = FlumeVAEEncoder(fallback_to_hash=True)
    63→        assert encoder.EMBEDDING_DIM == 256
    64→        result = encoder.encode("test")

    68→class TestJourneyTrackerFLUMEPath:
    69→    """Test journey_tracker with optional FLUME encoder."""
    70→
    71→    def test_flume_path_produces_2048d(self):
    72→        """When FLUME encoder is available, text_to_latent still returns 2048D."""
    73→        from cohezion.compound.journey_tracker import JourneyTracker
    74→
    75→        tracker = JourneyTracker()
    76→
    77→        # Mock the FLUME encoder
    78→        mock_encoder = MagicMock()
    79→        mock_encoder.encode.return_value = np.random.randn(256).astype(np.float32)
    80→        mock_encoder.is_available.return_value = True
    81→        tracker._flume_encoder = mock_encoder
    82→
    83→        result = tracker.text_to_latent("deploy the API")
    84→        assert result.shape == (2048,)
    85→
    86→    def test_flume_paraphrases_closer_than_unrelated(self):
    87→        """FLUME path should make paraphrases closer than unrelated texts."""
    88→        from cohezion.compound.journey_tracker import JourneyTracker
    89→
    90→        tracker = JourneyTracker()
    91→
    92→        # Mock FLUME encoder that preserves semantic similarity
    93→        rng = np.random.RandomState(42)
    94→
    95→        def mock_encode(text: str) -> np.ndarray:
    96→            # Paraphrases get similar vectors (varied, not constant)
    97→            if "deploy" in text.lower() or "deployment" in text.lower():
    98→                base = rng.randn(256).astype(np.float32)
    99→                base[:128] += 2.0  # Strong positive signal in first half
   100→            elif "test" in text.lower():
   101→                base = rng.randn(256).astype(np.float32)
   102→                base[128:] += 2.0  # Strong positive signal in second half
   103→            else:
   104→                base = rng.randn(256).astype(np.float32)
   105→            base /= np.linalg.norm(base)
   106→            return base
   107→
   108→        mock_encoder = MagicMock()
   109→        mock_encoder.encode.side_effect = mock_encode
   110→        mock_encoder.is_available.return_value = True
   111→        tracker._flume_encoder = mock_encoder
   112→
   113→        v1 = tracker.text_to_latent("deploy the API")
   114→        v2 = tracker.text_to_latent("API deployment")
   115→        v3 = tracker.text_to_latent("run the tests")
   116→
   117→        sim_paraphrase = np.dot(v1, v2) / (np.linalg.norm(v1) * np.linalg.norm(v2))
   118→        sim_unrelated = np.dot(v1, v3) / (np.linalg.norm(v1) * np.linalg.norm(v3))
   119→
   120→        assert sim_paraphrase > sim_unrelated
   121→
   122→    def test_hash_fallback_when_no_flume(self):
   123→        """Without FLUME encoder, should use existing hash path."""
   124→        from cohezion.compound.journey_tracker import JourneyTracker
   125→
   126→        tracker = JourneyTracker()
   127→        # No _flume_encoder set → uses hash path
   128→        result = tracker.text_to_latent("deploy the API")
   129→        assert result.shape == (2048,)
   130→

    12→class TestKLAnnealing:
    13→    """Test KL annealing schedule."""
    14→
    15→    def test_beta_starts_at_zero(self):
    16→        """Beta should start at 0 at step 0."""
    17→        from cohezion.flume.train_vae import kl_annealing_beta
    18→
    19→        assert kl_annealing_beta(step=0, warmup_steps=100, max_beta=0.1) == 0.0
    20→
    21→    def test_beta_reaches_max_after_warmup(self):
    22→        """Beta should reach max_beta at warmup_steps."""

    24→
    25→        beta = kl_annealing_beta(step=100, warmup_steps=100, max_beta=0.1)
    26→        assert abs(beta - 0.1) < 1e-6
    27→
    28→    def test_beta_stays_at_max_after_warmup(self):

    43→class TestActiveUnits:
